  x: B.B. Lal's excavation at Hastinapur (1950-52) established the Painted Grey Ware as a culture.
[g07c1d1380c65] sub1
  From which place was the copper found at Painted Grey Ware sites possibly obtained?
  Kolar | *Khetri | Singhbhum | Zawar
  x: The copper objects of the PGW sites possibly came from Khetri in Rajasthan.
[nc505b378b32] sub2
  What was iron called in the Later Vedic texts?
  Lohita ayas | *Krishna ayas | Hiranya | Nishka
  x: Iron is called krishna ayas or shyama ayas ('black metal') in Later Vedic texts. Lohita ayas denotes copper, plain ayas in the Rigveda meant copper or bronze, hiranya is gold and nishka was a gold ornament later used as a unit of value.
[nf91779d988f] sub2
  In which period did iron technology emerge in North India?
  1500 and 1200 BCE | *1000 and 800 BCE | 1200 and 1000 BCE | 600 and 400 BCE
  x: Iron technology in North India is dated to about 1000-800 BCE and was common at most Painted Grey Ware sites.
[n6fbc98e5b45] sub2
  Which archaeological site in Krishnagiri district, Tamil Nadu, has yielded iron AMS-dated to about 2172 BCE?
  *Mayiladumparai | Adichanallur | Thirumalapuram | Sivagalai
  x: Mayiladumparai in Krishnagiri district has iron AMS-dated to 2172 BCE, the earliest claim before Sivagalai. Sivagalai (Thoothukudi) is now the oldest at about 2953 BCE; Thirumalapuram (Tenkasi) has an Iron Age urn chamber and Adichanallur has urn burials.
[n21e6f13bd52] sub2
  Consider the following statements about the iron dates from Tamil Nadu:
1. They establish that Painted Grey Ware originated in Tamil Nadu.
2. They suggest iron use in India began much earlier than the Gangetic date of about 1000 BCE.
Which of the above statements is/are correct?
  1 only | *2 only | Both 1 and 2 | Neither 1 nor 2
  x: The Mayiladumparai (2172 BCE) and Sivagalai (about 2953 BCE) dates push Indian iron use far earlier than about 1000 BCE; they say nothing about the origin of Painted Grey Ware.
[f0dcdd0a924d8] sub2
  Which metal is meant by 'lohita ayas' in the Vedic texts?
  Iron | Gold | *Copper | Tin
  x: Lohita ayas was copper; krishna or shyama ayas was iron and hiranya was gold.
[f7db121a8c734] sub2
  What did the term 'ayas' denote in the Rigveda?
  *Copper or bronze | Iron | Silver | Gold
  x: Rigvedic ayas meant copper or bronze; iron was called krishna ayas in Later Vedic texts.
[f2fefa60b6514] sub2
  Which Tamil Nadu site has yielded iron dated to about 2953 BCE, now the oldest?
  Mayiladumparai | Adichanallur | Thirumalapuram | *Sivagalai
  x: Sivagalai in Thoothukudi district yielded iron dated to about 2953 BCE, older than Mayiladumparai's 2172 BCE.
[f8252c769ae3b] sub2
  Which of the following is NOT an early iron site of North India?
  Atranjikhera | Kaushambi | *Adichanallur | Hastinapur
  x: Adichanallur is an urn-burial site in Thoothukudi, Tamil Nadu; the others are North Indian iron sites.
[faa8bf32b47b7] sub2
  Besides krishna ayas, by which other name was iron called in the Later Vedic texts?
  *Shyama ayas | Lohita ayas | Hiranya | Nishka
  x: Iron was krishna or shyama ayas, the 'black metal'; lohita ayas was copper.
[f3bfc17940e96] sub2
  Consider the following statements about iron in the Later Vedic period:
1. Iron axes and fire helped clear the Gangetic forests.
2. Taxila in Gandhara has yielded iron of about 1000 BC.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
  1 only | 2 only | *Both 1 and 2 | Neither 1 nor 2
  x: Iron axes with fire cleared the Gangetic forests, and Taxila is listed among the early iron sites (c. 1000 BC).
[g0ef419b9154d] sub2
  Which was the first metal used by the people of India?
  *Copper | Iron | Gold | Silver
  x: Copper was the first metal used in India, in the Chalcolithic (copper-stone) age; iron came later.
[gaefb19f6e87e] sub2
  What does the term 'Chalcolithic' mean?
  Black metal age | *Copper-stone age | New stone age | Iron-bronze age
  x: Chalcolithic comes from the Greek khalkos (copper) and lithos (stone): people used copper and stone tools together.
[nb0961bac923] sub2
  With which of the following is Adichanallur associated?
  Painted Grey Ware | Ochre Coloured Pottery | *Urn burials | Fire altars
  x: Adichanallur is associated with urn burials and is not a Painted Grey Ware site, unlike Atranjikhera, Hastinapur and Bhagwanpura.
[n7b538fb7dd3] sub3
  Consider the following statements about the Iron Age in Andhra Pradesh:
1. It is associated with the Megalithic culture and Black and Red Ware pottery.
2. It is unrelated to megalithic burial traditions.
Which of the above statements is/are correct?
  *1 only | 2 only | Both 1 and 2 | Neither 1 nor 2
  x: The Iron Age in Andhra Pradesh is associated with the Megalithic culture and Black and Red Ware, with sites such as Nagarjunakonda cited in this context.
[n2a5b6284e02] sub3
  The emergence of the ‘second urbanisation’ is associated with which pottery phase and which group of settlements?
  Black and Red Ware; Videha and Kekaya | *Northern Black Polished Ware; Shravasti, Kausambi and Hastinapur | Painted Grey Ware; Kurukshetra and Bhagwanpura | Red Ware; Punjab and Sarasvati
  x: True towns emerged after the Vedic period during the Northern Black Polished Ware phase, including Shravasti, Kausambi and Hastinapur.
[n54ebaf7dfa5] sub3
  What was rice called in the Later Vedic period?
  Yava | *Vrihi | Godhuma | Tila
  x: Rice (vrihi) became a chief crop with ritual importance, while barley continued to be grown.
[n4b91a499b72] sub3
  What is the largest number of oxen mentioned for yoking a plough in the Later Vedic period?
  8 | 12 | 16 | *24
  x: Ploughs were described as yoked with 6, 8, 12 and even 24 oxen, showing the growing scale of plough agriculture.
[n9d80bc507cc] sub3
  Which of the following describes the settlements of the Later Vedic period?
  Large cities with extensive burnt-brick architecture were common. | *Villages with mud and wattle houses predominated, while true towns emerged later. | All settlements were fortified imperial capitals. | Nomadic camps were the only form of settlement.
  x: Settlements were mainly villages with mud and wattle houses. Burnt bricks were not prevalent, and true towns emerged after the Vedic period during the NBPW phase.
[n844fffb470e] sub3
  Who were the 'Shresthins' in the Later Vedic period?
  *Groups of merchants | Royal bodyguards | Forest-dwelling ascetics | Village priests
  x: Shresthins point to merchant groups. Nishka functioned as a unit of value, while barter continued as the main form of exchange.
[f0db3ab1cdb37] sub3
  Which of the following is NOT correct about Later Vedic agriculture?
  Rice was called vrihi | Wheat was grown | Barley cultivation continued | *Rice had no ritual importance
  x: Rice (vrihi) was the main crop and had ritual importance; wheat was grown and barley continued.
[f62e375bd73f0] sub3
  Consider the following statements about Later Vedic settlements:
1. They were mainly villages of mud and wattle houses.
2. Burnt bricks were commonly used.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
  *1 only | 2 only | Both 1 and 2 | Neither 1 nor 2
  x: Settlements were mainly villages of mud and wattle houses; burnt bricks were not common.
[g19371cf600fe] sub3
  Which of the following is an Iron Age Megalithic site of Andhra with Black and Red Ware?
  Bhagwanpura | Atranjikhera | *Nagarjunakonda | Noh
  x: The Iron Age in Andhra is the Megalithic culture with Black and Red Ware, seen at sites like Nagarjunakonda; the others are PGW sites.
[g99a8f9fe4934] sub3
  According to H.D. Sankalia, in which era was rice first harvested in Andhra Pradesh?
  Palaeolithic era | Mesolithic era | Harappan era | *Iron Age (Megalithic) era
  x: Sankalia held that rice was first harvested in Andhra Pradesh in the Iron Age Megalithic era.
[n8b4b526fd25] sub4
  Which Veda is associated with the Adhvaryu priest?
  Rigveda | *Yajurveda | Atharvaveda | Samaveda
  x: The Yajurveda gives ritual procedures in prose, is linked with the Adhvaryu and has Krishna and Shukla branches.
[nindian-history-56-12] sub4
  What was the role of the Adhvaryu priest in the Vedic sacrifice?
  Recited the Rigvedic hymns | *Handled the practical, manual work of the sacrifice | Taught the Veda and Vedangas for a living | Served as the royal charioteer
  x: The Adhvaryu handled the manual work of the sacrifice; the Hotri recited hymns, the Upadhyaya taught and the Suta was the charioteer.
[fcd3a52602813] sub4
  Which Vedic priest chanted the Samaveda hymns set to music?
  Hotri | Adhvaryu | Brahman | *Udgatri
  x: The Samaveda sets Rigvedic hymns to music, chanted by the Udgatri; the Adhvaryu belongs to the Yajurveda.
[n6b4503250c3] sub4
  Which god became supreme in the Later Vedic period?
  Indra | *Prajapati | Varuna | Agni
  x: In the Later Vedic period Indra and Agni lost prominence and Prajapati became supreme.
[na0047a7f9c5] sub4
  The Upanishadic thinkers criticised which tendency of Later Vedic religion?
  *Elaborate ritualism | Image worship in temples | Worship of mother goddesses | Tree worship
  x: Later Vedic worship became centred on elaborate rituals, which prompted the Upanishadic critique.
[n41947f6a0e1] sub4
  Which one of the following pairs is NOT correctly matched?
  Rudra — God of animals who later merged with Shiva | Prajapati — Creator and supreme god | Vishnu — Protector or preserver | *Pushan — God of warriors and destroyer of cattle
  x: Pushan became associated with cattle and was regarded as the god of the Shudras. The other three pairs are correctly matched.
[ff1fb2f112132] sub4
  In the Later Vedic period, Pushan was regarded as the god of which varna?
  Brahmanas | Kshatriyas | Vaishyas | *Shudras
  x: Pushan, a cattle god, came to be regarded as the god of the Shudras.
[f8719f58d0eab] sub4
  Which god, minor in the Rigveda, rose as preserver in the Later Vedic period?
  *Vishnu | Rudra | Indra | Agni
  x: Vishnu rose as preserver; Indra and Agni lost importance and Rudra was the god of animals.
[f094f50bd84fd] sub4
  Which Later Vedic god of animals later merged with Shiva?
  Pushan | Prajapati | *Rudra | Varuna
  x: Rudra, god of animals, later merged with Shiva.
[nc0b15b72567] sub4
  In Later Vedic sacrifice, which priest chanted the Samaveda?
  *Udgatri | Adhvaryu | Hotri | Upadhyaya
  x: The Udgatri chanted the Samaveda; the Hotri recited the Rigveda and the Adhvaryu performed sacrificial acts.
[f37531ad38b64] sub4
  What was the role of the Hotri priest in the Later Vedic sacrifice?
  *Recited the Rigveda | Chanted the Samaveda | Performed sacrificial acts | Taught for a fee
  x: The Hotri recited the Rigveda; the Udgatri chanted the Samaveda and the Adhvaryu performed the acts.

=== NOTES 1:19 | Later Vedic Polity – Territorial Kingship, Royal Sacrifices, Ratnins
## SUB 0 | Kingdoms and Philosopher-Kings
[19:0:0] TABLE head: Kingdom | Region
  [19:0:0.0] Kuru | Delhi–Meerut / upper Doab; Hastinapur, Kurukshetra
  [19:0:0.1] Panchala | Middle Doab–Rohilkhand; Ahichchhatra
  [19:0:0.2] Kosala | Eastern UP
  [19:0:0.3] Kashi | Varanasi region
  [19:0:0.4] Videha | North Bihar, beyond the Sadanira (Gandak); King Janaka
[19:0:1] - Kuru-Panchala dominated the upper Ganga valley; **Kosala, Kashi and Videha** then rose in the east → Mahajanapadas.
[19:0:2] # Upanishadic (Kshatriya scholar) kings – they taught Brahmins
[19:0:3] TABLE head: King | Kingdom
  [19:0:3.0] Janaka | Videha
  [19:0:3.1] Ashvapati | Kekaya
  [19:0:3.2] Ajatashatru | Kashi (not the Magadha king)
  [19:0:3.3] Pravahana Jaivali | Panchala
[19:0:4] ~~ Sources: PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 1 p.131, p.140; old app notes 1:18, 1:19
## SUB 1 | Royal Sacrifices – Rajasuya, Ashvamedha, Vajapeya
[19:1:0] TABLE head: Sacrifice | Purpose
  [19:1:0.0] Rajasuya | Royal consecration; conferred supreme power; includes the Ratnahavimshi (offerings at the Ratnins' houses)
  [19:1:0.1] Ashvamedha | Horse sacrifice; supremacy over other kings and control of the area the horse roamed
  [19:1:0.2] Vajapeya | "Drink of strength"; chariot race won by the king over his kinsmen
  [19:1:0.3] Punarabhisheka | Second anointing at the end of the Rajasuya
[19:1:1] - **Muvendavelan** is a title (Chola-period chief), **not** a royal sacrifice.
[19:1:2] - Large **Shrauta** sacrifices needed many priests and heavy **dakshina** (up to 2,40,000 cows in a Rajasuya) → strengthened the **Brahmanas**.
[19:1:3] - **AP angle:** Satavahanas, **Ikshvakus**, Vishnukundins performed these rites; Ikshvaku titles: **Vajapeya = Samrat/Virat; Ashvamedha = Swarat**; an **Ashvamedha altar at Nagarjunakonda**.
[19:1:4] > Ashvamedha = over other kings; Vajapeya = over kinsmen; Rajasuya = consecration.
[19:1:5] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.31; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 1 p.134, p.140; old app notes 1:19
## SUB 2 | Assemblies and Taxes
[19:2:0] - **Vidatha disappeared**; **Sabha and Samiti continued** but declined, dominated by chiefs, nobles and Brahmanas; **women no longer sat** in the Sabha; village assemblies tried local cases.
[19:2:1] TABLE head: Tax | Nature
  [19:2:1.0] Bali | Tribute: voluntary in the Rigveda, now regular and compulsory – the king's tax on the people
  [19:2:1.1] Bhaga | King's share of produce (one-sixth; texts give 1/6 to 1/2)
  [19:2:1.2] Shulka | Customs / tolls
[19:2:2] - **Vaishyas** were the main (only) **tribute-payers**; Bhagadugha collected, Sangrahitri managed the revenue.
[19:2:3] - Varman = armour, Vidatha = assembly – not taxes.
[19:2:4] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.31-32; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 1 p.134-136; old app notes 1:19
## SUB 3 | From Tribe to Territory – Kingship
[19:3:0] - **Jana** (tribe) → **janapada / rashtra** (territory); the word **rashtra** first appears in the Later Vedic texts; tribal chiefdom → **hereditary territorial monarchy**, which prepared the way for the **Mahajanapadas** (6th century BCE).
[19:3:1] - Kingship **hereditary**, usually to the **eldest son** (primogeniture); men became rajas by performing big sacrifices; traces of election survived.
[19:3:2] - **Ekarat** (Atharvaveda, Aitareya Brahmana) = **sole / paramount ruler**.
[19:3:3] TABLE head: Region (Aitareya Brahmana) | Ruler's title
  [19:3:3.0] East | Samrat
  [19:3:3.1] South | Bhoja
  [19:3:3.2] West | Svarat
  [19:3:3.3] North | Virat
[19:3:4] - State forms in the Aitareya Brahmana: **Rajya, Bhojya, Swarajya, Vairajya, Samrajya** – **Ganatantra is NOT** one.
[19:3:5] # Divine origin of kingship
[19:3:6] TABLE head: Text | Statement
  [19:3:6.0] Aitareya Brahmana | Earliest account: the gods, losing to the Asuras, chose a king to lead them; king = protector of Brahmins
  [19:3:6.1] Taittiriya Brahmana | Theory of the divine origin of kingship
  [19:3:6.2] Shatapatha Brahmana | Linked the king with Prajapati; Rajasuya and Vajapeya gave him divine qualities
[19:3:7] - Kingship was **not** traced to election by village assemblies.
[19:3:8] TABLE head: Early Vedic | Later Vedic
  [19:3:8.0] Tribe; cattle; elective chief | Territory; agriculture; hereditary king
  [19:3:8.1] Voluntary bali; strong assemblies | Compulsory taxes (bali, bhaga, shulka); assemblies decline; Ratnins
[19:3:9] > East = Samrat, North = Virat; rashtra first appears now.
[19:3:10] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.30-31; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 1 p.134, p.141; old app notes 1:19
## SUB 4 | Ratnins and Officials
[19:4:0] - **Ratnins ("jewels")** = the king's council of **12** royal functionaries: purohita, rajan, mahishi, senani, bhagadugha, sangrahitri, suta, kshata, govikartana, gramani, parivakti, akshavapa (some add palagala); honoured in the **Ratnahavimshi** rite of the Rajasuya.
[19:4:1] TABLE head: Official | Function
  [19:4:1.0] Purohita | Chief priest
  [19:4:1.1] Senani | Army chief
  [19:4:1.2] Suta | Charioteer and herald
  [19:4:1.3] Bhagadugha | Revenue collector (king's share)
  [19:4:1.4] Sangrahitri | Treasurer
  [19:4:1.5] Akshavapa | Accountant; superintendent of dice
  [19:4:1.6] Palagala | Messenger
[19:4:2] - More officers but no regular bureaucracy and **no standing army**.
[19:4:3] > Bhagadugha collects; Sangrahitri stores.
[19:4:4] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.31; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 1 p.135; old app notes 1:19

  x: Pravahana Jaivali ruled Panchala; Ashvapati ruled Kekaya, Janaka Videha and Ajatashatru Kashi.

  x: Ashvapati ruled Kekaya. Janaka ruled Videha, Ajatashatru was associated with Kashi and Pravahana Jaivali with Panchala.
[nfa68effa0d7] sub0
  Which development contributed to the emergence of the Mahajanapadas?
  The exclusive expansion of Kuru power into the Deccan | *The rise of Kosala, Kashi and Videha in the east after Kuru–Panchala dominance in the upper Ganga valley | The replacement of agriculture by maritime trade in North India | The disappearance of all territorial kingdoms in favour of tribal units
  x: The Kuru–Panchala alliance dominated the upper Ganga valley, while Kosala, Kashi and Videha rose in the east. This political development contributed to the emergence of the Mahajanapadas.
[n308ec8f5dbf] sub0

  x: The Kuru kingdom was associated with the Delhi–Meerut or upper Doab region, with Hastinapur and Kurukshetra. Panchala was linked with Ahichchhatra, Kashi with Varanasi, and Videha with North Bihar.

  x: The Vajapeya included the chariot race; the Rajasuya was a consecration and the Ashvamedha a horse sacrifice asserting supremacy.
[n67069b045b9] sub1

  Abolished hereditary kingship | Transferred all military authority to village assemblies | *Required many priests and involved heavy dakshina | Excluded priests from royal ceremonies
  x: Large Shrauta sacrifices required numerous priests and substantial dakshina, thereby increasing Brahmanical influence and strengthening the ritual basis of royal power.

  What was the share of produce taken by the king as Bhaga?

  x: Bhaga represented the king’s share and was fixed at one-sixth of the produce. Vaishyas were the tribute-payers.
[ndda274eb6f9] sub2
  Which of the following correctly lists the taxes that became compulsory in the Later Vedic period?

  x: The compulsory taxes were Bali, a tribute; Bhaga, the king’s share; and Shulka, customs or tolls.

  *Customs or tolls | Tribute | King's share of produce | Land grant
  x: Shulka was customs or tolls; bali was tribute and bhaga the king's one-sixth share.

  x: The Vidatha disappeared, while the Sabha and Samiti continued under the control of chiefs and wealthy nobles.

  x: Shulka was a customs duty or toll. Bali was a regular tribute and Bhaga the king's share of produce.

  What did Later Vedic society become, in contrast to the tribe-based Early Vedic society?
  Tribe-based with voluntary bali | Nomadic and pastoral | Urban and based on coined money | *Territory-based with agriculture and compulsory taxes
  x: The Early Vedic phase meant tribe, cattle and voluntary bali; the Later Vedic phase meant territory, agriculture and compulsory taxes.
[n28b284eec78] sub3

  Republican guilds | Colonial provinces | *Mahajanapadas | Urban corporations

[n7d256d0ce9f] sub3
  Which term referring to territory appears for the first time in the Later Vedic political context?
  Vana | *Rashtra | Pura | Shreni
  x: The term rashtra, meaning territory, first appears in this political context and reflects the rise of territorial kingship.
[n74a243e98b3] sub3
  Which of the following is NOT one of the state forms named in the Aitareya Brahmana?
  Bhojya | Vairajya | Rajya | *Ganatantra
  x: The Aitareya Brahmana names Rajya, Bhojya, Swarajya, Vairajya and Samrajya. Ganatantra is not included in this list.
[n06bec5f7117] sub3
  In the Later Vedic period, who usually succeeded to the throne?
  The youngest son | *The eldest son | The king's nephew | The Purohita's nominee
  x: Later Vedic kingship became generally hereditary, with succession usually favouring the eldest son, though traces of election continued.
[nddae75f5e49] sub3
  What was the ruler of the eastern region called in the Later Vedic texts?
  *Samrat | Bhoja | Svarat | Virat
  x: The eastern ruler was Samrat, the southern Bhoja, the western Svarat and the northern Virat.
[ffb6795504fd4] sub3
  What is the meaning of 'Ekarat' in the Aitareya Brahmana?
  Elected chief | *Sole ruler | Ruler of the south | Tribal priest
  x: Ekarat means the 'sole ruler', the paramount king, in the Aitareya Brahmana.
[fa9e0a9afe4c6] sub3
  According to the Aitareya Brahmana, what was the ruler of the northern region called?
  Samrat | Bhoja | Svarat | *Virat
  x: North = Virat; East = Samrat, South = Bhoja, West = Svarat.
[fdf0ecc013dc5] sub3
  According to the Aitareya Brahmana, what was the ruler of the southern region called?
  *Bhoja | Svarat | Samrat | Virat
  x: The southern ruler was Bhoja; the western ruler was Svarat.
[g847a70db0288] sub3
  According to the Aitareya Brahmana, what was the ruler of the western region called?
  *Svarat | Samrat | Bhoja | Virat
  x: The Aitareya Brahmana calls the ruler of the west Svarat; east Samrat, south Bhoja, north Virat.
[n829b972fc29] sub3
  According to the Aitareya Brahmana, how did kingship originate?
  The villages appointed a ruler to supervise agriculture | *The gods, losing to the Asuras, chose a king to lead them | The Asuras selected a king to defeat the gods | The Brahmanas elected a king to collect customs duties
  x: The Aitareya Brahmana gives an early account in which the gods, suffering defeat at the hands of the Asuras, selected a king as their war leader. This linked kingship with the need for military leadership and divine sanction.
[n6ffd485e399] sub3
  Which Brahmana regarded the king as a protector of Brahmins?
  Shatapatha Brahmana | Gopatha Brahmana | *Aitareya Brahmana | Taittiriya Brahmana
  x: The Aitareya Brahmana regarded the king as protector of Brahmins; Brahmanical ritual monopoly supported royal legitimacy.
[ned965954faf] sub3
  Consider the following statements about the Shatapatha Brahmana's theory of kingship:

2. It regarded the Rajasuya and Vajapeya as giving the king divine qualities.
3. It traced kingship exclusively to election by village assemblies.
Which of the above statements are correct?
  *1 and 2 only | 2 and 3 only | 1 and 3 only | 1, 2 and 3
  x: The Shatapatha Brahmana linked the king to Prajapati and saw the Rajasuya and Vajapeya as conferring divine qualities; it did not trace kingship to village election.
[n325a799a64a] sub3
  Which of the following belongs to the Later Vedic polity rather than the Early Vedic polity?
  *Ratnins | An elective chief | Voluntary bali | Strong assemblies
  x: Ratnins were part of the Later Vedic polity; an elective chief, voluntary bali and strong assemblies marked the Early Vedic polity.
[ncd307e03556] sub4
  Which one of the following officials was responsible for collecting the king’s share of revenue?
  Sangrahitri | Palagala | *Bhagadugha | Akshavapa
  x: Bhagadugha was the revenue or tax collector who collected the king’s share. Sangrahitri was the treasurer, Akshavapa the accountant and superintendent of dice, and Palagala the messenger.
[n5b55be6a0ad] sub4
  Who was the Sangrahitri in the Later Vedic polity?
  Army chief | *Treasurer | Chief priest | Charioteer and royal herald
  x: The Sangrahitri was the treasurer; the Senani was army chief, the Purohita chief priest and the Suta charioteer and herald.
[n4b6b12e3d87] sub4
  Who were the 'Ratnins' in the Later Vedic polity?
  A body of twelve military commanders | *A council of twelve royal functionaries | A group of twelve provincial governors | A group of twelve village assemblies
  x: The Ratnins, or ‘jewels’, were the king’s council of twelve functionaries. They were honoured during the Ratnahavimshi rite of the Rajasuya.
[nf0a1a0a3b89] sub4

  x: The Ratnahavimshi rite involved offerings at the houses of the Ratnins and honoured the king’s twelve functionaries.

  Which Ratnin was the charioteer and herald?
  Senani | *Suta | Palagala | Akshavapa
  x: The Suta was charioteer and herald; Senani was army chief, Palagala messenger and Akshavapa accountant.
[ff47cac3fc181] sub4
  How many Ratnins made up the king's council in the Later Vedic period?

  x: The Ratnins ('jewels') were a council of 12 officials honoured in the Ratnahavimshi rite.

  Which Ratnin served as the army chief in the Later Vedic polity?

  x: The Senani was the army chief; the Purohita was chief priest and the Palagala the messenger.
[f6d076f61d1c5] sub4

  x: The Akshavapa was the accountant, linked with dice; the Sangrahitri was treasurer.

  Purohita – Chief priest | Palagala – Messenger | *Bhagadugha – Treasurer | Suta – Charioteer
  x: Bhagadugha was the revenue collector; the treasurer was the Sangrahitri.

=== NOTES 1:20 | Later Vedic Society – Varnas, Ashramas, Gotra, Status of Women
## SUB 0 | Varna Order, Untouchability and Guilds
[20:0:0] - Society stratified in the order **Brahmana, Kshatriya, Vaishya, Shudra** = the **Varna system** (varna came to rest on **birth**; spread of jatis is post-Vedic).
[20:0:1] TABLE head: Varna | Role
  [20:0:1.0] Brahmana | Priests, teachers; most powerful
  [20:0:1.1] Kshatriya (Rajanya) | Rulers, warriors
  [20:0:1.2] Vaishya | Farmers, herders, traders, artisans; only tribute-payers
  [20:0:1.3] Shudra | Service; no upanayana, no Vedas, no Sabha
[20:0:2] - First statement of the four varnas: **Purusha Sukta, Rigveda 10th Mandala**; **Dvija** (twice-born) = upper three, entitled to upanayana.
[20:0:3] - **Aitareya Brahmana**: Shudra = "**servant of another**", could be expelled or beaten at will.
[20:0:4] - Outside the varna order: **Vratyas, Nishadas**.
[20:0:5] - Varna + ashrama = **Varnashrama Dharma**.
[20:0:6] # Untouchability
[20:0:7] - **Chhandogya Upanishad**: the **Chandala** as a person of despised birth – an early sign only; untouchability was absent in the Rigvedic age.
[20:0:8] - Untouchability took **clear, formal shape in the Dharmashastra phase** (early Christian era), not in the Rigvedic or Later Vedic phase.
[20:0:9] # Guilds and money-lending
[20:0:10] - **Sarthavaha** (caravan leader, head of a trade guild / Srenipramukh) = **Vaishya**; guild rules = **Samay, Srenidharma**; Vaishya guilds (gana) headed by a **Shreshthin**.
[20:0:11] - First reference to **money-lending**: **Shatapatha Brahmana**; usurer = **kusidin**.
[20:0:12] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.29, p.32; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 1 p.135-137; old app notes 1:20
## SUB 1 | Ashramas, Purusharthas and Pancha Mahayajnas
[20:1:0] TABLE head: Ashrama | Duty
  [20:1:0.0] Brahmacharya | Student; Vedic study
  [20:1:0.1] Grihastha | Householder; daily Pancha Mahayajnas
  [20:1:0.2] Vanaprastha | Gradual withdrawal (forest)
  [20:1:0.3] Sannyasa | Complete renunciation; added last
[20:1:1] - **Four ashramas** are described; they are the custom that **emerged in the post-Vedic (Later Vedic) period** – the varnas go back to the Rigveda's Purusha Sukta and Indra-Surya-Rudra-Maruts are Rigvedic gods.
[20:1:2] - Only the first three were established in Later Vedic times: first three in the **Chhandogya Upanishad**; all four first in the **Jabala Upanishad** (PMF: early Dharmasutras).
[20:1:3] - **Pancha Mahayajnas:** **Brahma** (Vedic study), **Deva** (gods), **Pitri** (ancestors), **Bhuta** (living beings), **Nri / Atithi** (hospitality to guests).
[20:1:4] - **Purusharthas:** Dharma (duty), Artha (wealth), Kama (desire) = **trivarga**; **Moksha** (liberation) added later under Upanishadic ideas.
[20:1:5] ~~ Sources: PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 1 p.136; old app notes 1:20
## SUB 2 | Gotra and Kinship
[20:2:0] - **Gotra** literally = **cow-pen** (word first in the **Rigveda**, meaning cow shelter); as a **clan descended from a common male ancestor** it was established in **Later Vedic (post-Vedic) times**; the Atharvaveda mentions gotra in the lineage sense.
[20:2:1] - **Gotra exogamy**: no marriage within the same gotra (with caste endogamy and **sapinda** rules).
[20:2:2] - **Kula** = family; **Vamsha** = lineage; **Gotra** = clan; **Kosa = treasury (odd one out)**.
[20:2:3] - **Irawati Karve**: *Kinship Organization in India* (1953) – four kinship zones.
[20:2:4] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.29, p.32; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 1 p.136; old app notes 1:20
## SUB 3 | Samskaras, Upanayana and Education
[20:3:0] - **Samskaras = 16** (shodasha): 3 before birth (Garbhadhana, Pumsavana, Simantonnayana), Jatakarma, Chudakarma (tonsure, 3rd year) … Upanayana, Vivaha, Antyeshti.
[20:3:1] - **Upanayana** = sacred-thread initiation into Vedic study for the **upper three varnas**, in the boy's **8th year**; full description in the **Shatapatha Brahmana**; **Shudras and women excluded**.
[20:3:2] TABLE head: Varna | Upanayana metre
  [20:3:2.0] Brahmana | Gayatri
  [20:3:2.1] Kshatriya | Trishtubh
  [20:3:2.2] Vaishya | Jagati
[20:3:3] - **Gurukula**: the pupil **lived with the guru**.
[20:3:4] TABLE head: Teacher | Meaning
  [20:3:4.0] Acharya | Initiated the pupil; taught the whole Veda free
  [20:3:4.1] Upadhyaya | Taught Veda / Vedangas for a livelihood
[20:3:5] > Kshatriya = Trishtubh; Upadhyaya paid, Acharya free.
[20:3:6] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.31-32; Rocket Indian History Part 1 p.117; old app notes 1:20
## SUB 4 | Forms of Marriage
[20:4:0] TABLE head: Form | Nature
  [20:4:0.0] Brahma | Duly dowered daughter given to a Veda-learned man of good conduct
  [20:4:0.1] Daiva | Daughter given to the officiating priest as fee
  [20:4:0.2] Arsha | Groom gives a token cow and bull
  [20:4:0.3] Prajapatya | No dowry or bride price; couple jointly perform dharma
  [20:4:0.4] Asura | Marriage by purchase (bride price)
  [20:4:0.5] Gandharva | Mutual love / consent
  [20:4:0.6] Rakshasa | Marriage by capture
  [20:4:0.7] Paishacha | Seduction of a sleeping or drunk girl; lowest
[20:4:1] - **8 forms** listed by Manu and Kautilya; first four approved.
[20:4:2] - **Anuloma** = higher-varna man + lower-varna woman; **Pratiloma** = lower-varna man + higher-varna woman (disapproved).
[20:4:3] - **Satavahana Andhra (AP angle):** Gautamiputra Satakarni claimed to protect varnashrama dharma; anuloma offspring = **Ugra**, pratiloma offspring = **Chandala**.
[20:4:4] > Asura = purchase; Rakshasa = capture; Gandharva = love.
[20:4:5] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.33; old app notes 1:20
## SUB 5 | Status of Women
[20:5:0] - **Declined**: lost upanayana and political rights (barred from the **Sabha**), excluded from most rituals, could not choose husbands; child marriage appears; symbolic widow self-immolation (sati as a practice is later).
[20:5:1] TABLE head: Text | Statement
  [20:5:1.0] Aitareya Brahmana | Daughter = source of misery; son = protector
  [20:5:1.1] Shatapatha Brahmana | Wife is half her husband
  [20:5:1.2] Maitrayani Samhita | Liquor, dice and women = three evils
  [20:5:1.3] Brihadaranyaka Upanishad | Gargi and Maitreyi debated with Yajnavalkya in Janaka's court
[20:5:2] - Learned women of Vedic literature: **Gargi, Maitreyi** (Upanishadic); Apala, Ghosha, Lopamudra, Vishvavara (Rigvedic hymn-composers).
[20:5:3] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.32-33; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 1 p.138, p.149, p.151; old app notes 1:20

[f1e8eb103d294] sub3
  Which metre was prescribed for the Upanayana of a Vaishya?
  Gayatri | Trishtubh | Anushtubh | *Jagati
  x: Vaishya = Jagati; Brahmana = Gayatri; Kshatriya = Trishtubh.
[n717bfa1ee53] sub3
  Which of the following pairs of varna and Upanayana metre is correctly matched?
  *Kshatriya – Trishtubh | Brahmana – Jagati | Vaishya – Gayatri | Brahmana – Trishtubh
  x: The Kshatriya Upanayana was associated with Trishtubh. The Brahmana's metre was Gayatri and the Vaishya's Jagati.
[f773c5c416adc] sub3
  Which teacher taught the Veda and Vedangas for a livelihood?
  *Upadhyaya | Acharya | Purohita | Hotri
  x: The Upadhyaya taught for a livelihood, while the Acharya taught the whole Veda free.
[n05d6aafc6d0] sub4
  In Satavahana Andhra, what were the offspring of an anuloma union and a pratiloma union called, respectively?
  Acharya and Upadhyaya | Chandala and Ugra | *Ugra and Chandala | Purohita and Adhvaryu
  x: Anuloma offspring were called Ugra, whereas pratiloma offspring were called Chandala. The other pairs refer either to teachers and priests or reverse these two designations.
[nf8564dc47c1] sub4
  Which Satavahana ruler claimed to have protected varnashrama dharma?
  Simuka | Hala | Yajna Sri Satakarni | *Gautamiputra Satakarni
  x: Gautamiputra Satakarni claimed to have protected varnashrama dharma.
[fead5e8b15676] sub4
  In Satavahana Andhra, what was the offspring of a pratiloma union called?
  *Chandala | Nishada | Ugra | Vratya
  x: Pratiloma offspring were called Chandala and anuloma offspring Ugra.
[nf73906f31cb] sub4
  In the Arsha form of marriage, what did the groom give the bride's father?
  A bride price in gold | A pair of horses | *A token cow and bull | A share of his land
  x: In the Arsha form, the groom gave a token cow and bull to the bride's father.
[n77139d7a125] sub4
  Which one of the following was regarded as the lowest form of marriage in the Dharmashastra classification?
  Daiva | Prajapatya | *Paishacha | Arsha
  x: Paishacha, involving the seduction of a sleeping or intoxicated girl, was regarded as the lowest form of marriage.
[nab777c7a3f3] sub4
  Payment of a bride price characterised which form of marriage?
  *Asura | Rakshasa | Prajapatya | Gandharva
  x: Bride price characterised Asura marriage; Gandharva marriage was based on mutual consent.
[nebb1187b168] sub4
  Which of the following is NOT correctly matched?
  *Rakshasa — marriage by purchase | Gandharva — marriage by mutual consent | Paishacha — seduction of a sleeping or intoxicated girl | Asura — payment of bride price
  x: Rakshasa marriage involved capture or abduction. Marriage by purchase was known as Asura marriage.
[f497b21ecf418] sub4
  How many forms of marriage are listed by Manu and Kautilya?
  4 | 6 | *8 | 16
  x: Manu and Kautilya list eight forms of marriage, of which the first four were approved.
[f7c4cbb2896bb] sub4
  Which form of marriage was marriage by capture of the bride?
  Asura | *Rakshasa | Paishacha | Gandharva
  x: Rakshasa marriage was by capture; Asura was by purchase.
[f405df9484478] sub4
  Which form of marriage was marriage by mutual consent?
  *Gandharva | Prajapatya | Daiva | Brahma
  x: Gandharva marriage was based on mutual consent.
[f38bd5c4b4c64] sub4
  Between whom was an anuloma marriage?
  A lower-varna man and a higher-varna woman | Members of the same varna only | Members of the same gotra | *A higher-varna man and a lower-varna woman
  x: Anuloma was a higher-varna man with a lower-varna woman; pratiloma, the reverse, was disapproved.
[f111354a37d94] sub4
  In which form of marriage was the daughter given to the officiating priest?
  Brahma | Arsha | *Daiva | Prajapatya
  x: In Daiva marriage the daughter was given to the officiating priest.
[gd06835e46ac6] sub4
  In which form of marriage was there no dowry, with the couple jointly performing their dharma?
  Arsha | Asura | Brahma | *Prajapatya
  x: In the Prajapatya form there was no dowry and the couple jointly performed dharma.
[ge79c384661c1] sub4
  Consider the following statements:
1. The Brahma, Daiva, Arsha and Prajapatya forms of marriage were the approved forms.
2. Pratiloma marriage, between a lower-varna man and a higher-varna woman, was approved.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
  *1 only | 2 only | Both 1 and 2 | Neither 1 nor 2
  x: The first four forms were approved; pratiloma marriage was disapproved.
[n1b3a702e5cb] sub5
  Which initiation ceremony did women lose the right to in the Later Vedic period?
  Annaprashana | *Upanayana | Namakarana | Vivaha
  x: The status of women declined through loss of Upanayana, restricted education and exclusion from religious and public activities.
[n453e1c110b7] sub5

  Compulsory female military service and polyandry | *Child marriage and sati | Abolition of marriage and communal ownership | Female kingship and universal widow remarriage
  x: References to child marriage and sati appear in accounts of the declining status of women in Later Vedic society.
[f3bee0152fe55] sub5
  Which text describes a daughter as a cause of misery and a son as the protector?
  Shatapatha Brahmana | Jabala Upanishad | Chhandogya Upanishad | *Aitareya Brahmana
  x: The Aitareya Brahmana says a daughter is a source of misery; the Shatapatha calls the wife half her husband.
[f3b588705dc22] sub5
  In which text is the statement 'the wife is half her husband' found?
  *Shatapatha Brahmana | Aitareya Brahmana | Brihadaranyaka Upanishad | Atharvaveda
  x: The Shatapatha Brahmana describes the wife as half her husband.
[fd74bfc77c028] sub5

  1 and 2 only | 2 and 3 only | 1 and 3 only | *1, 2 and 3
  x: Women lost upanayana, were barred from the Sabha and public rites, and child marriage and sati appear.
[gf1d8c062a2e4] sub5
  Who among the following women debated with Yajnavalkya in the Brihadaranyaka Upanishad?
  *Gargi | Apala | Ghosha | Lopamudra
  x: The Brihadaranyaka Upanishad records the debate of the woman scholar Gargi with Yajnavalkya.

=== NOTES 1:21 | Vedic Literature – Vedas, Brahmanas, Aranyakas, Upanishads, Vedangas, Sutras
## SUB 0 | Epics, Puranas and Smritis
[21:0:0] # Mahabharata
[21:0:1] - By **Vyasa**; original **Jaya / Jayasamhita** (8,800 shlokas) → Bharata → **Shatasahasri Samhita** (~1 lakh verses), **18 books (parvas)**, **Harivamsa** appended; older than the Ramayana; Bhagavad Gita (part of it) in **Sanskrit**.
[21:0:2] - **Vaisampayana** told it to **Janamejaya**; **Ugrashrava Sauti** narrated it to Shaunaka's sages at **Naimisharanya**.
[21:0:3] - War at Kurukshetra for **18 days**; Arjuna's grandfather = **Vichitravirya** (Pandu's nominal father); Karna spent Indra's **Vasavi Shakti** on **Ghatotkacha**.
[21:0:4] # Ramayana
[21:0:5] - By **Valmiki**: **24,000** verses, 7 kandas, five stages from the 5th century BCE; Rama first met Hanuman in the **Kishkindha Kanda**.
[21:0:6] - **Janaka**'s brother = **Kushadhvaja** (king of Sankashya); Janaka's family priest-guru = **Shatananda**, son of sage **Gautama** (the Upanishadic Janaka learnt from Yajnavalkya).
[21:0:7] - **Samudra manthan:** serpent **Vasuki** was the churning rope.
[21:0:8] # Puranas and Smritis
[21:0:9] - **18 Mahapuranas**; five marks (**pancha-lakshana**): **Sarga, Pratisarga**, Vamsa, Manvantara, Vamshanucharita. Complete **Dasavatara** narration: **Bhagavata (Maha-bhagavatam)**.
[21:0:10] - Yugas: **Krita (Satya) → Treta → Dvapara → Kali**.
[21:0:11] - **Manu** = first law-giver (Manusmriti; disapproves **widow remarriage**); **Mitakshara** on the Yajnavalkya Smriti = **Vijnaneshwara**.
[21:0:12] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.34; old app notes 1:21; Valmiki Ramayana Bala Kanda (Shatananda, Kushadhvaja)
## SUB 1 | Four Vedas – Content, Priests, Trayi
[21:1:0] TABLE head: Veda | Content | Priest | Upaveda
  [21:1:0.0] Rigveda | Hymns (prayers); oldest / earliest | Hotri (Hota) | Ayurveda
  [21:1:0.1] Yajurveda | Sacrificial formulas; rites and rituals of yajnas (verse + prose) | Adhvaryu | Dhanurveda
  [21:1:0.2] Samaveda | Rigvedic hymns set to melody; shortest; origin of Indian music | Udgatri | Gandharvaveda
  [21:1:0.3] Atharvaveda | Magical charms, spells, healing; latest | Brahman | Shilpaveda (architecture) / Arthashastra
[21:1:1] - **Samaveda** is **compiled from the Rigveda**: about 1,549 verses (1,875 with repeats), all but about **75** from the Rigveda (mostly Mandalas VIII and IX).
[21:1:2] - **Atharvaveda**: earliest charms and spells; **Ayurveda ("Science of Life") first appears** here (its prime origin; PMF lists Ayurveda as Upaveda of the Rigveda); term **kshetra-patni ("mistress of the field")** originates here (UPSC 2026); Sabha and Samiti = "twin daughters of **Prajapati**"; mentions Pashupati; recensions **Paippalada, Shaunaka**; reflects non-Aryan beliefs.
[21:1:3] - **Trayi / Vedatrayi** = **Rig, Yajur, Sama**; Atharvaveda excluded (recognised later).
[21:1:4] - **Yajurveda** has two branches, **Krishna (Black)** and **Shukla (White)**; first mentions Rajasuya and Vajapeya.
[21:1:5] - **Upavedas:** Ayurveda – medicine; Dhanurveda – warfare; Gandharvaveda – music; **Shilpaveda – architecture** (not astronomy).
[21:1:6] - Vedic people were skilled in **astronomy** (Vedanga Jyotisha), **mathematics** (Sulbasutra geometry) and **medicine** (Atharvaveda) – **not chemistry**.
[21:1:7] > Hotri-Adhvaryu-Udgatri-Brahman = Rig-Yajur-Sama-Atharva; Trayi excludes Atharva.
[21:1:8] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.21-23, p.29; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 1 p.147-148; old app notes 1:21; UPSC CSE Prelims 2026 key (Vajiram & Ravi)
## SUB 2 | Upanishads and Vedanta
[21:2:0] - **108** (Muktika list), about **13 principal**; c. 800–600 BCE; **anti-ritualistic**; **Atman = Brahman**; karma, samsara, moksha; called **Vedanta** ("end of the Vedas").
[21:2:1] - **Prasthanatrayi** = **Upanishads, Brahma Sutra** (Badarayana), **Bhagavad Gita**; the **Bhagavata is not** included. **Shankaracharya** wrote bhashyas on these three – **not on the Rigveda**.
[21:2:2] TABLE head: Saying / story | Upanishad
  [21:2:2.0] Satyameva Jayate (national motto, adopted 26 Jan 1950, below the State Emblem) | Mundaka
  [21:2:2.1] Asato ma sadgamaya; Yajnavalkya with Gargi and Maitreyi; first statement of transmigration | Brihadaranyaka
  [21:2:2.2] Tat tvam asi; Satyakama Jabala (son of the unmarried Jabala); Uddalaka–Shvetaketu; Krishna, son of Devaki (first mention) | Chandogya
  [21:2:2.3] Nachiketa–Yama dialogue on death | Katha (Kathopanishad)
  [21:2:2.4] Trimurti | Maitrayani
  [21:2:2.5] Name means "white horse"; introduces bhakti | Shvetashvatara
  [21:2:2.6] Taittiriya Upanishad | Krishna Yajurveda
[21:2:3] > Satyameva Jayate = Mundaka (not Mandukya); Jabala Upanishad is a different, later text.
[21:2:4] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.24-25; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 1 p.149-152; old app notes 1:21
[21:2:5] - **Pancha kosha** (five sheaths: anna, prana, mano, vijnana, ananda) comes from the **Taittiriya Upanishad**; in education it is elaborated by **Sri Aurobindo** (integral education).
## SUB 3 | Rigveda – Mandalas, Hymns, Gayatri
[21:3:0] - **10 mandalas, 1,028 hymns (suktas)** = 1,017 + 11 Valakhilya; 10,552 mantras; a **collection of hymns** to nature deities; similar to the Iranian **Avesta**; "First Testament of Mankind".
[21:3:1] TABLE head: Mandala | Content
  [21:3:1.0] II–VII | Family books, oldest core (Gritsamada, Vishvamitra, Vamadeva, Atri, Bharadvaja, Vasishtha)
  [21:3:1.1] III | Gayatri Mantra – composed by Vishvamitra, deity Savitri
  [21:3:1.2] VII | Battle of Ten Kings (Vasishtha)
  [21:3:1.3] IX | Wholly Soma (Soma Pavamana, 114 hymns)
  [21:3:1.4] X | Purusha Sukta (four varnas, origin of caste – first use of "varna" for the four orders), Nasadiya Sukta (creation), Surya Sukta (marriage, X.85)
[21:3:2] - Word **gotra** first occurs in the **Rigveda**, meaning cow shelter / herd of cows.
[21:3:3] - **Memory of the World (2007):** **30 Rigveda manuscripts** at the **Bhandarkar Oriental Research Institute, Pune** on UNESCO's register; Vedic chanting = UNESCO intangible heritage (2008).
[21:3:4] - **Script:** the Rigveda was composed and transmitted **orally**; exam keys give **Brahmi** as its basic script (surviving manuscripts are much later, in Devanagari / Sharada).
[21:3:5] > Gayatri = Mandala III, Vishvamitra; Soma = Mandala IX; Purusha Sukta = Mandala X.
[21:3:6] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.22-23, p.29; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 1 p.145-147; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.1; old app notes 1:21
## SUB 4 | Mahabharata – Characters, Epithets and Episodes
[21:4:0] # Narrators and scribe
[21:4:1] - **Ganesha** wrote the Mahabharata down as **Vyasa** dictated it (tradition, Adi Parva).
[21:4:2] - **Sanjaya**, given divine sight by Vyasa, narrated the war to the blind king **Dhritarashtra** (the Bhagavad Gita is his report).
[21:4:3] - **Vishnu Sahasranama** was first recited by **Bhishma** to Yudhishthira (Anushasana Parva).
[21:4:4] # Who's who
[21:4:5] TABLE head: Person | Remember
  [21:4:5.0] Bhishma (Devavrata) | Son of King Shantanu and the river Ganga
  [21:4:5.1] Karna | Radheya (after foster mother Radha); led the Kaurava army for two days (16th–17th) after Drona
  [21:4:5.2] Arjuna | Gudakesha ("conqueror of sleep")
  [21:4:5.3] Sahadeva | Youngest Pandava; killed Shakuni on the 18th day
  [21:4:5.4] Nakula | Conch Sughosha (Sahadeva – Manipushpaka; Krishna – Panchajanya; Arjuna – Devadatta; Bhima – Paundra)
[21:4:6] - **Dushta chatushtaya** (the four wicked): **Duryodhana, Dussasana, Karna, Shakuni** – Sisupala is not one of them.
[21:4:7] > Endowment-exam angle: epithet → hero, weapon/conch → owner, "who killed whom".
[21:4:8] ~~ Sources: Mahabharata (BORI Critical Edition) Adi, Karna, Shalya and Anushasana Parvas; Bhagavad Gita 1.14–1.18 (conches); old app notes 1:21
## SUB 5 | Yajurveda Branches and the Brahmanas
[21:5:0] TABLE head: Branch | Nature | Samhitas | Brahmana
  [21:5:0.0] Shukla (White) | Mantras only, clearly arranged | Vajasaneyi (Madhyandina, Kanva) | Shatapatha
  [21:5:0.1] Krishna (Black) | Mantras + prose mixed | Taittiriya, Maitrayani, Kathaka, Kapishthala | Taittiriya
  [21:5:0.2] Veda | Brahmanas | Aranyakas | Upanishads
  [21:5:0.3] Rig | Aitareya, Kaushitaki | Aitareya, Kaushitaki | Aitareya, Kaushitaki
  [21:5:0.4] Sama | Panchavimsha (Tandya), Jaiminiya, Shadvimsha, Chandogya | Jaiminiya, Chandogya | Chandogya, Kena
  [21:5:0.5] Yajur | Taittiriya, Shatapatha | Taittiriya, Brihadaranyaka | Brihadaranyaka, Isha, Katha, Taittiriya (Krishna YV), Shvetashvatara
  [21:5:0.6] Atharva | Gopatha (only one) | None | Mundaka, Mandukya, Prashna
[21:5:1] - **Shatapatha Brahmana**: largest; Videgha Madhava's eastward journey; **Great Flood**; **first reference to money-lending** (kusidin); full **upanayana**; "wife is half her husband"; **Purushamedha** (described with the Vajasaneyi Samhita of the Shukla Yajurveda).
[21:5:2] - **Aitareya Brahmana**: origin of kingship, ten forms of government, Shudra as servant, first mention of the **Andhras** (Sunahshepa story).
[21:5:3] > Gopatha = Atharva; Jaiminiya = Sama; Purushamedha = Shatapatha.
[21:5:4] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.23-24, p.29; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 1 p.144, p.147-148; Rocket Indian History Part 1 p.117; old app notes 1:21
## SUB 6 | Bhagavata Purana, Sages and Puranic Stories
[21:6:0] - **Shuka** (son of Vyasa) narrated the **Bhagavata Purana** to King **Parikshit**; Suta later retold it to Shaunaka's sages.
[21:6:1] - **Ekavimsati** (21) in the Bhagavatam = the **21 avataras** of Vishnu listed in its first skandha.
[21:6:2] - **Durvasa** = son of sage **Atri** (one of the Saptarishis) and **Anasuya** (with Dattatreya and Chandra).
[21:6:3] - **Vaikhanasa** = a **maharshi** to whom the Vaikhanasa Agama of temple worship is traced.
[21:6:4] - **Dhruva** = son of King **Uttanapada** and Suniti (Vishnu and Bhagavata Puranas).
[21:6:5] - **Samudra manthan** of the Kshira Sagara: Mount **Mandara** = churning rod (serpent Vasuki = rope).
[21:6:6] - **Gurus:** **Shukracharya** (son of Bhrigu) = guru of the asuras/rakshasas; **Brihaspati** = guru of the devas.
[21:6:7] - **Vasishtha's cow** = **Nandini** (daughter of Kamadhenu) in most books and keys; the Valmiki Ramayana calls her **Shabala**.
[21:6:8] ~~ Sources: Bhagavata Purana 1.3 (avataras), 1.4 and 4.8 (Dhruva), 8.6–8.7 (churning); Vishnu Purana 1.11; Mahabharata Adi Parva (Nandini); old app notes 1:21
## SUB 7 | Vedangas, Sutras and Grammarians
[21:7:0] TABLE head: Vedanga | Subject
  [21:7:0.0] Shiksha | Phonetics
  [21:7:0.1] Kalpa | Rituals (Sutras)
  [21:7:0.2] Vyakarana | Grammar (Panini)
  [21:7:0.3] Nirukta | Etymology (Yaska)
  [21:7:0.4] Chandas | Metrics (Pingala)
  [21:7:0.5] Jyotisha | Astronomy
[21:7:1] - Mimamsa is a darshana, **not** a Vedanga.
[21:7:2] TABLE head: Sutra | Subject
  [21:7:2.0] Shrauta | Public, large sacrifices
  [21:7:2.1] Grihya | Domestic rites
  [21:7:2.2] Dharma | Law, social practice
  [21:7:2.3] Sulba | Measurement and construction of altars (geometry)
[21:7:3] - Sulba authors: **Baudhayana, Apastamba, Katyayana**, Manava. **Baudhayana theorem** = lengths of the sides of a right-angled triangle = the **Pythagoras theorem**.
[21:7:4] TABLE head: Author | Work
  [21:7:4.0] Panini (c. 500 BCE) | Ashtadhyayi (Sanskrit grammar)
  [21:7:4.1] Yaska | Nirukta
  [21:7:4.2] Katyayana | Varttika
  [21:7:4.3] Patanjali | Mahabhashya; Yoga Sutras
[21:7:5] - Darshana founders: Nyaya – Gautama; **Vaisheshika – Kanada**; Sankhya – Kapila; Yoga – Patanjali; Purva Mimamsa – Jaimini; Uttara Mimamsa – Badarayana.
[21:7:6] - **Gayatri chandas** = **24 syllables** (3 × 8).
[21:7:7] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.25; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.1-5; old app notes 1:21
## SUB 8 | Ramayana – Characters and Episodes
[21:8:0] TABLE head: Episode | Answer
  [21:8:0.0] Woke Rama with "Kausalya supraja Rama" (Bala Kanda) | Vishwamitra
  [21:8:0.1] Mother of Shatrughna (twin of Lakshmana) | Sumitra (Kaikeyi – Bharata; Kausalya – Rama)
  [21:8:0.2] Urged Ravana to abduct Sita | His sister Surpanakha, insulted by Lakshmana
  [21:8:0.3] Ravana's son killed by Hanuman (Sundara Kanda) | Aksha Kumara (Indrajit was killed by Lakshmana)
  [21:8:0.4] Sabari served in the ashram of | Sage Matanga, near Pampa
  [21:8:0.5] Real name of Indrajit | Meghanada (killed by Lakshmana)
[21:8:1] ~~ Sources: Valmiki Ramayana – Bala Kanda 23, Aranya Kanda 34–35 and 74, Sundara Kanda 47; old app notes 1:21
## SUB 9 | Layers of Vedic Literature – Shruti
[21:9:0] - Order: **Samhitas → Brahmanas → Aranyakas → Upanishads**; Vedangas, Sutras, Smritis come later.
[21:9:1] - All are **Shruti** ("heard"), **apaurusheya** (not of human origin); Smriti = remembered (Dharmashastras, epics, Puranas).
[21:9:2] - **Samhitas + Brahmanas = Karma-kanda**; **Aranyakas + Upanishads = Jnana-kanda**.
[21:9:3] - **Brahmanas**: prose commentaries that **explain the Vedic hymns** and the meaning of sacrifices (karma-marga; "liturgies").
[21:9:4] - **Aranyakas**: "**forest texts**", concluding parts of the Brahmanas; symbolic / secret meaning of ritual; bridge karma-marga → jnana-marga. **Atharvaveda has no Aranyaka**.
[21:9:5] - **Veda Vyasa (Krishna Dvaipayana)** compiled and classified the Vedas. Veda from *vid* = to know.
[21:9:6] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.21-24; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 1 p.143, p.149, p.152; old app notes 1:21

  1 and 3 only | *1 and 2 only | 2 and 3 only | 1, 2 and 3
  x: The Ramayana has about 24,000 verses composed in five stages from the 5th century BCE; about one lakh verses in 18 books describes the Mahabharata.

[n90a3f9149dd] sub1
  Which Vedic text contains spells and charms against disease?
  *Atharvaveda | Yajurveda | Samaveda | Rigveda
  x: The Atharvaveda contains spells and charms; the Samaveda sets Rigvedic hymns to music and the Yajurveda gives ritual procedures.
[f6662b87a3f6e] sub1
  Which is regarded as the latest Veda?
  Rigveda | Samaveda | Yajurveda | *Atharvaveda
  x: The Atharvaveda, with spells and charms against disease, is the latest Veda.
[n169fb71fae7] sub1
  Which of the following pairs is NOT correctly matched?
  *Shilpa Veda — Astronomy | Gandharva Veda — Music | Dhanurveda — Warfare | Ayurveda — Medicine
  x: Shilpa Veda deals with architecture, not astronomy. The other three pairs correctly describe the Upavedas.
[na2c6996ab20] sub1
  Which of the following pairs of Veda and priest is correctly matched?
  Rigveda – Adhvaryu | Yajurveda – Brahman | Atharvaveda – Hotri | *Samaveda – Udgatri
  x: The Samaveda was chanted by the Udgatri. The Rigveda belongs to the Hotri, the Yajurveda to the Adhvaryu and the Atharvaveda to the Brahman priest.
[na5e0c446c1c] sub1
  Which one of the following is regarded as the latest among the four Vedas?
  Samaveda | Yajurveda | *Atharvaveda | Rigveda
  x: Among the four Vedas, the Atharvaveda is regarded as the latest composition. The Rigveda is regarded as the oldest.
[f28d23afa9f46] sub1
  Which Upaveda is associated with the Atharvaveda?
  Ayurveda | Dhanurveda | Gandharvaveda | *Shilpaveda
  x: Shilpaveda is the Upaveda of the Atharvaveda; Ayurveda goes with the Rigveda.
[fe6a709a798d0] sub1
  Which is the shortest of the four Vedas?
  Rigveda | Yajurveda | Atharvaveda | *Samaveda
  x: The Samaveda, Rig hymns set to melody, is the shortest Veda.
[f56705792d94c] sub1

  It is the latest of the four Vedas | Its priest is the Brahman | *It is included in the Trayi | Gopatha is its only Brahmana
  x: The Trayi comprises the Rig, Yajur and Sama Vedas; the Atharvaveda is excluded.
[nindian-history-51-2] sub1
  Which of the following pairs of Veda and content is correctly matched?
  Rigveda – Sacrificial formulas and procedures | *Atharvaveda – Spells and charms | Samaveda – Prose sacrificial formulas | Yajurveda – Melodies for chanting
  x: The Atharvaveda contains spells and charms. The Rigveda is a collection of hymns, the Yajurveda contains sacrificial formulas and procedures, and the Samaveda sets Rigvedic verses to melody for chanting.

  x: Shilpaveda is architecture, not astronomy.

  Under which category are the Puranas classified?
  Shruti | Vedanga | Aranyaka | *Smriti
  x: The Puranas are Smriti - later, legend-based works - whereas the Upanishads are Shruti.

=== NOTES 1:22 | Vedic Religion, Ritual and Philosophy; the Six Orthodox Schools
## SUB 0 | Six Orthodox Schools – Founders, Texts and Pairs
[22:0:0] TABLE head: School (darshana) | Founder | Key text | Core idea
  [22:0:0.0] Samkhya (oldest) | Kapila | Samkhya Sutra; Samkhya Karika (Ishvarakrishna) | Dualism of Purusha and Prakriti; Satkaryavada
  [22:0:0.1] Yoga | Patanjali | Yoga Sutra | Ashtanga (eight-limbed) discipline; Samkhya metaphysics
  [22:0:0.2] Nyaya | Gautama (Akshapada) | Nyaya Sutra | Logic; four pramanas; 16 padarthas
  [22:0:0.3] Vaisheshika | Kanada | Vaisheshika Sutra | Atomism (paramanu); 9 dravyas
  [22:0:0.4] Purva Mimamsa | Jaimini | Mimamsa Sutra | Vedic ritual and duty (karma)
  [22:0:0.5] Uttara Mimamsa (Vedanta) | Badarayana (Vyasa) | Brahma Sutra | Brahman the only reality; knowledge of Brahman
[22:0:1] - **Three pairs:** **Samkhya–Yoga, Nyaya–Vaisheshika, Mimamsa–Vedanta**.
[22:0:2] - **Oldest darshana = Samkhya** (APPSC 2018 flashcard). Kapila is its **earliest exponent**.
[22:0:3] - **Uttara Mimamsa = Badarayana = Vyasa** (APPSC G1 2002) – not Kapila, Patanjali or Gautama; an old APPSC key giving Kapila is wrong.
[22:0:4] - **Not among the six:** **Smriti** (a class of texts), **Lokayata** (Charvaka materialism), **Kapalika** (a Shaiva sect) (UPSC 2014), Charvaka, Ajivika.
[22:0:5] - **Fully articulated / perfected in the Gupta age** (SSC CPO 2003); their sutras are older.
[22:0:6] - **Navya-Nyaya** ("new logic") was founded by **Gangesha Upadhyaya** of Mithila (Tattvachintamani, 14th c.); Raghunatha Shiromani came later.
[22:0:7] - **Kapila and the sons of Sagara:** in the Ramayana and Puranas the sage **Kapila** burnt the 60,000 sons of King Sagara to ashes; Bhagiratha brought the Ganga down to redeem them.
[22:0:8] > Founder matches: Kapila–Samkhya, Patanjali–Yoga, Gautama–Nyaya, Kanada–Vaisheshika, Jaimini–Purva Mimamsa, Badarayana–Uttara Mimamsa.
[22:0:9] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.25, p.36-37; PMF Art & Culture Part 5 p.97-102; NextGen Indian History Part 3 (Guptas); Rocket Indian History Part 1 p.121; Britannica 'Nyaya'
## SUB 1 | Rigvedic Gods – Indra, Agni, Varuna, Soma
[22:1:0] TABLE head: God | Domain | Exam fact
  [22:1:0.0] Indra | Lightning, storm, war | ~250 hymns (most); ranked first; Purandara (fort-breaker); most popular god; wife Shachi (Indrani)
  [22:1:0.1] Agni | Fire | ~200 hymns (second); messenger between gods and men; first hymn of the Rigveda (1.1)
  [22:1:0.2] Varuna | Water, cosmic order | Ranked third; upholder of Rita; ethically the highest
  [22:1:0.3] Soma | Plant and ritual drink | Whole 9th Mandala; god of plants; Haoma of the Avesta
  [22:1:0.4] Surya | Sun | Aspects: Savitri (Gayatri), Mitra, Pushan, Vishnu
  [22:1:0.5] Pushan | Paths, herdsmen, cattle | Solar aspect; god of the Shudras in later Vedic times
  [22:1:0.6] Brihaspati | Prayer | Priest (purohita) and preceptor of the gods
  [22:1:0.7] Dyaus | Sky | Celestial father, like Zeus
  [22:1:0.8] Ushas | Dawn | Female
  [22:1:0.9] Aditi | Mother of the gods | Female; not Prithvi (Prithvi = fertility goddess)
  [22:1:0.10] Aranyani | Forest | Female
  [22:1:0.11] Rudra | Disease | Archer god of the mountains; later merged with Shiva
  [22:1:0.12] Yama | Death | –
  [22:1:0.13] Vishnu | Aspect of Surya | Only 3 hymns; rose later
[22:1:1] - **War-god** = Indra (UPPSC 2011, Uttarakhand 2016). When Indra is not offered, the most hymns = **Agni** (UPPSC 2010).
[22:1:2] - **Priest of the gods** = **Brihaspati** in most keys; Agni is also hailed as purohita in Rigveda 1.1.
[22:1:3] - **Later Vedic:** **Prajapati** became supreme creator; **Vishnu** rose as preserver; **Rudra** grew; **Indra and Agni lost importance**.
[22:1:4] > Indra 250 > Agni 200 > Varuna third; 9th Mandala = Soma; Aditi (not Prithvi) = mother of gods.
[22:1:5] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.28, p.34-36; Britannica 'Brihaspati', 'Indra'; old app notes row 22
## SUB 2 | Vedanta Schools – Shankara, Ramanuja, Madhva
[22:2:0] TABLE head: School | Teacher | Meaning | Path to mukti
  [22:2:0.0] Advaita | Adi Shankaracharya (8th c., Kaladi, Kerala) | Non-dualism: Brahman alone is real, Atman = Brahman, world = Maya | Jnana (knowledge)
  [22:2:0.1] Vishishtadvaita | Ramanujacharya (11th c., Sriperumbudur; Srirangam) | Qualified non-dualism: souls and world real, inseparable from Vishnu | Bhakti and divine grace
  [22:2:0.2] Dvaita | Madhvacharya | Dualism | Bhakti
  [22:2:0.3] Dvaitadvaita | Nimbarka | Dualistic non-dualism | –
  [22:2:0.4] Shuddhadvaita | Vallabhacharya | Pure non-dualism | –
[22:2:1] - **Shankara:** founded **four mathas** – **Sringeri** (south), **Puri** (east), **Dwarka** (west), **Badrinath / Jyotirmath (Joshimath)** (north); not Prayag, Kanchi or Varanasi. Called a "**crypto-Buddhist**" (Buddhist in disguise). Also Dashanami order and Panchayatana worship.
[22:2:2] - **Ramanuja:** first great promoter of the **Bhakti** movement; Sri Vaishnava.
[22:2:3] - **Bhagavad Gita:** gives **equal importance to Jnana, Karma and Bhakti** as means of liberation (UPPSC 2005).
[22:2:4] - **Swami Vivekananda's Neo-Vedanta** reworked Advaita.
[22:2:5] - Other teachers: **Lakulisha** – Pashupata Shaivism; **Nagarjuna** – Madhyamika (Shunyavada).
[22:2:6] > Advaita = Shankara = non-dualism = jnana; Vishishtadvaita = Ramanuja = bhakti; Dvaita = Madhva.
[22:2:7] ~~ Sources: PMF Art & Culture Part 5 p.98, p.105-110; Britannica 'Bhagavadgita'; old app notes row 22
## SUB 3 | Mimamsa – Jaimini, Kumarila Bhatta, Karma and Apurva
[22:3:0] - **Founder:** **Jaimini** (Mimamsa Sutra). Other exponents: **Kumarila Bhatta** and **Prabhakara**.
[22:3:1] - **Kumarila Bhatta** = **Purva Mimamsa** (APPSC G2 2012, 2019; AP History PYQs) – not Uttara Mimamsa, Advaita, Dvaita, Yogachara or Jnana marga. Works: **Slokavartika**, Tantravartika. He **defended Vedic ritual (karma-kanda)** and attacked Buddhist doctrines.
[22:3:2] - Also called **Karma-Mimamsa**: it centres on ritual action → the **theory of karma** belongs to Mimamsa (UPPSC 1997).
[22:3:3] - **Liberation through karma** – correct performance of Vedic rites and duties (UPSC 2005).
[22:3:4] - The **Vedas are eternal** and contain eternal truth (CGPSC 2013).
[22:3:5] - **Apurva** = the **unseen force** that links a ritual act (karma) with its later result.
[22:3:6] - **Purva Mimamsa = Karmakanda** (ritual part of the Vedas); **Uttara Mimamsa = Jnanakanda** (knowledge part).
[22:3:7] ~~ Sources: PMF Art & Culture Part 5 p.100-101; Rocket Indian History Part 1 p.121; NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.37; old app notes row 22
## SUB 4 | Doctrines of the Schools – Samkhya, Nyaya, Vaisheshika
[22:4:0] TABLE head: Doctrine | School
  [22:4:0.0] Satkaryavada (effect pre-exists in the cause) | Samkhya (MPPSC 2025)
  [22:4:0.1] World formed by the blending of nature (Prakriti) | Samkhya
  [22:4:0.2] Atom as the basis of the world | Vaisheshika (Kanada)
  [22:4:0.3] Pramanas – perception, inference, comparison, testimony | Nyaya
  [22:4:0.4] Apurva; theory of karma | Mimamsa
  [22:4:0.5] Niyati (predetermined fate) | Ajivika (Makkhali Gosala)
  [22:4:0.6] Materialism | Lokayata / Charvaka
  [22:4:0.7] Syadvada | Jaina
  [22:4:0.8] Shunyavada / Madhyamika | Nagarjuna
[22:4:1] - **Samkhya:** **accepts rebirth**; liberation by **self-knowledge** (discriminating Purusha from Prakriti), not by an outside agent → UPSC 2013 "only 2". Kapila taught that man's life is shaped by the **forces of nature, not a divine agency** (CGPSC 2020: both true).
[22:4:2] - **Nyaya:** "nyaya" = logic or decision; **16 padarthas**. **Vaisheshika:** 9 dravyas; beginning of physics in India.
[22:4:3] - **APPSC G1 2023 match:** Samkhya – blending of nature; Vaisheshika – atom; Lokayata – materialism; Ajivika – predetermined fate.
[22:4:4] ~~ Sources: PMF Art & Culture Part 5 p.99-104; NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.37-38; Rocket Indian History Part 1 p.121; old app notes row 22
## SUB 5 | Astika vs Nastika; Charvaka (Lokayata)
[22:5:0] - **Test:** **belief in the authority of the Vedas** – astika accept it, nastika reject it (UPPSC 2005); not belief in God, rebirth or heaven.
[22:5:1] - **Astika:** the six darshanas. **Nastika:** **Charvaka, Buddhism (Bauddha), Jainism, Ajivika**.
[22:5:2] - **Charvaka = Lokayata** ("of the common people"); credited to **Brihaspati**; **materialism**; **perception** the only valid knowledge; no soul, rebirth or afterlife.
[22:5:3] - **Ajita Kesakambali** – early materialist of the Buddha's time.
[22:5:4] > Odd-one-out lists slip a nastika school (Charvaka, Bauddha, Ajivika) among the six.
[22:5:5] ~~ Sources: PMF Art & Culture Part 5 p.97, p.105; old app notes rows 22-23
## SUB 6 | Nature of Vedic Religion – Rita, Dharma, Sacrifice
[22:6:0] - **Nature worship**; male gods dominate; worship for material gains; **sacrifice (yajna) central**, in the open air – **no temples, no idols** (UPPSC 1993: "sacrifice and rituals").
[22:6:1] - **Henotheism** (term of **Max Müller**): each god praised in turn as supreme.
[22:6:2] - **Rita** = cosmic and moral order, upheld by **Varuna**; **Dharma** = obligation and duty to oneself and others, grown from Rita → UPSC 2011 "both".
[22:6:3] - Iranian parallels: **Mitra–Mithra, Rita–Asha, Soma–Haoma**.
[22:6:4] TABLE head: Ritual / term | Associated with
  [22:6:4.0] Hotri (Rig), Adhvaryu (Yajur), Udgatri (Sama) | Priests
  [22:6:4.1] Ceremonial chanting | Samaveda
  [22:6:4.2] Agnistoma | Regular Soma sacrifice
  [22:6:4.3] Rajasuya, Vajapeya, Ashvamedha | Royal sacrifices
  [22:6:4.4] Grihya Sutras | Domestic rites
[22:6:5] - **Sanskaras: 16** = 3 before birth, 12 during life, **1 after death**.
[22:6:6] - **Later Vedic:** elaborate, costly sacrifices; Prajapati supreme; Indra and Agni decline; priests grow powerful.
[22:6:7] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.28, p.33; PMF Art & Culture Part 5 p.97; old app notes row 22
## SUB 7 | Upanishadic Philosophy
[22:7:0] TABLE head: Concept | Meaning
  [22:7:0.0] Brahman | Ultimate reality (cosmic principle)
  [22:7:0.1] Atman | The self – not body or mind
  [22:7:0.2] Karma | Links action with consequence
  [22:7:0.3] Samsara | Rebirth – transmigration first in the Brihadaranyaka
  [22:7:0.4] Moksha | Liberation – not a Vedic Samhita term; first discussed in the Upanishads
[22:7:1] - **Main theme = philosophy** (UPPSC 2004, UP Lower 1998); anti-ritualistic; **Atman is Brahman**.
[22:7:2] - Criticised **mechanical sacrifice**; stressed meditation and the guru-disciple relationship; **ignorance** = cause of sorrow.
[22:7:3] - **Katha (Katha) Upanishad:** **Nachiketa** questions **Yama** on death and the Atman (UPSC 1997).
[22:7:4] - Basic approach is universal – it does not reject other schools or scriptures.
[22:7:5] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian History - APPSC Exams Part 1 p.25, p.29; PMF Ancient & Medieval Part 2 p.2; old app notes row 22
## SUB 8 | Ashtanga Yoga
[22:8:0] TABLE head: No. | Limb | Group
  [22:8:0.0] 1 | Yama | Bahiranga (outer)
  [22:8:0.1] 2 | Niyama | Bahiranga
  [22:8:0.2] 3 | Asana | Bahiranga
  [22:8:0.3] 4 | Pranayama | Bahiranga
  [22:8:0.4] 5 | Pratyahara | Bahiranga (the bridge to the inner limbs)
  [22:8:0.5] 6 | Dharana | Antaranga (inner)
  [22:8:0.6] 7 | Dhyana | Antaranga
  [22:8:0.7] 8 | Samadhi | Antaranga
[22:8:1] - **Antaranga sadhana = Dharana, Dhyana, Samadhi** (MPPSC 2024 → Dharana). **Anusmriti** is NOT a limb (CGPSC 2015).
[22:8:2] - **Bahiranga = 5** (Yama to Pratyahara); **antaranga = 3**; **Pranayama** is the 4th limb.
[22:8:3] ~~ Sources: PMF Art & Culture Part 5 p.99-100; old app notes row 22

  x: Prabhakara, like Kumarila Bhatta, belonged to Purva Mimamsa.
[f50ce62eee016] sub3
  Which work was written by Kumarila Bhatta?
  Brahma Sutra | *Slokavartika | Mahabhashya | Yoga Sutra
  x: Kumarila Bhatta wrote the Slokavartika and Tantravartika.
[n40aaf85028b] sub4
  Which one of the following pairs is NOT correctly matched?
  Materialism — Lokayata | Atom as basis of the world — Vaisheshika | Niyati — Ajivika | *Pramanas — Samkhya
  x: Pramanas, or valid means of knowledge, are associated with Nyaya, not Samkhya. The other three pairs are correctly matched.
[g73d653f4ff7a] sub4
  How many categories (padarthas) does the Nyaya school recognise?
  9 | 7 | *16 | 25
  x: The Nyaya school of Gautama is based on logic, the pramanas and 16 categories.
[fb7caf00cea36] sub4
  Niyati (fate) is the central doctrine of which sect?
  *Ajivikas | Jainas | Charvakas | Buddhists
  x: Niyati belongs to the Ajivikas of Makkhali Gosala, not the Jainas.
[f7d1f59d18856] sub4
  The doctrine of pramanas is associated with which school?
  Samkhya | Yoga | Vaisheshika | *Nyaya
  x: Pramanas belong to Nyaya, not Samkhya.
[nindian-history-51-4] sub5
  Which of the following was Lokayata?
  One of the six orthodox systems | A Shaiva sect | *A heterodox materialist school | A branch of Mimamsa
  x: Lokayata is a heterodox materialist school; the six orthodox systems are Sankhya, Yoga, Nyaya, Vaisheshika, Mimamsa and Vedanta.
[n259e8b4db92] sub5
  Which one of the following groups consists entirely of nastika systems or traditions?
  Mimamsa, Vedanta and Vaisheshika | Nyaya, Charvaka and Vedanta | Samkhya, Yoga and Nyaya | *Charvaka, Buddhism, Jainism and Ajivika
  x: Charvaka, Buddhism, Jainism and Ajivika reject the authority of the Vedas and are classified as nastika traditions.
[f92053dae40fb] sub5
  To whom is Lokayata materialism credited?
  *Brihaspati | Kapila | Kanada | Makkhali Gosala
  x: Lokayata / Charvaka materialism is credited to Brihaspati.
[n26c21c97724] sub5
  How is a system that accepts the authority of the Vedas classified?
  Lokayata | Nastika | *Astika | Ajivika
  x: Astika denotes the systems that accept Vedic authority, including the six orthodox darshanas.
[n57200473e3b] sub5
  By what other name is the philosophy of the Charvakas known?
  *Lokayata | Niyativada | Syadvada | Satkaryavada
  x: The Charvaka philosophy is called Lokayata (APPSC 2025); it is materialist and accepts only perception. Niyati is the Ajivika doctrine of fate, Syadvada is Jain, and Satkaryavada belongs to Samkhya.
[n22b1775dfd5] sub5
  Which one of the following is correctly matched with Charvaka or Lokayata?
  *Brihaspati — materialism | Makkhali Gosala — strict fatalism | Mahavira — rejection of rebirth | Buddha — denial of perception as a source of knowledge
  x: Brihaspati is associated with Charvaka or Lokayata materialism. Charvaka held perception to be the only valid means of knowledge and denied soul, rebirth and afterlife.
[faf5201aa240b] sub5
  According to Charvaka, which is the only valid means of knowledge?
  *Perception | Inference | Testimony | Scripture
  x: Charvaka (Lokayata) accepts only perception and denies soul, rebirth and afterlife.
[gbc128b947991] sub5
  Which teacher of the Buddha's time was a materialist?
  Makkhali Gosala | *Ajita Kesakambali | Nanda Vachcha | Bavari
  x: Ajita Kesakambali was a materialist; Makkhali Gosala and Nanda Vachcha were Ajivikas.
[n394e38d5312] sub6
  On what was Rig Vedic religion centred?
  Temple idols | *Male nature gods and open-air yajnas | Animal worship | Mother Goddess figurines
  x: Rig Vedic religion featured male nature gods like Indra, Agni and Varuna and open-air yajnas, with no temples or idols.
[nad8ac0c2c3d] sub6
  Which of the following describes the religious outlook of the Rig Vedic people?
  Animism without distinct personal deities | Monotheism with exclusive worship of one god | Ancestor worship centred on royal tombs | *Naturalistic polytheism with each god praised as supreme in turn
  x: Rig Vedic religion was naturalistic polytheism. Its tendency to regard each god as supreme in turn is called Henotheism or Kathenotheism.
[n4aa70c19a79] sub6
  Which of the following is NOT correct about Rig Vedic worship?
  *Temples and idols formed the normal basis of worship | Worship was conducted through hymns and yajnas in the open air | Its principal aims included cattle, sons and victory | The cult of the domestic hearth was significant
  x: Temples and idols were absent in Rig Vedic worship, which used hymns and open-air yajnas for material ends such as cattle, sons and victory.
[gda8655cf975c] sub6
  What is the Rigvedic practice of praising each god in turn as the supreme one called?
  Monotheism | *Henotheism | Monism | Animism
  x: Rigvedic religion was henotheistic (kathenotheism): each god was praised as supreme in turn.
[n938b1915f51] sub6
  Which of the following pairs correctly represents Iranian parallels of Rigvedic concepts?
  *Mitra–Mithra and Rita–Asha | Agni–Ahura and Soma–Indra | Varuna–Mitra and Rita–Haoma | Dyaus–Asha and Surya–Mithra
  x: Mitra corresponds to Mithra, while Rita has the Iranian parallel Asha. Haoma is the Iranian parallel of Soma, not Rita.
[f65f4dde70fc6] sub6
  Who applied the term 'henotheism' to Rigvedic religion?
  *Max Müller | William Jones | James Prinsep | Alexander Cunningham
  x: Max Müller used 'henotheism': each god in turn is praised as if supreme.
[f567767a8e727] sub6
  What does Rita mean in Rigvedic religion?
  Sacrificial fee | Rebirth | *Cosmic and moral order | Ritual drink
  x: Rita is the cosmic and moral order upheld by Varuna; dharma later grew from it.
[f42f12430b320] sub6

1. Prajapati became the supreme god.
2. Indra and Agni gained importance.
3. Worship centred on elaborate, costly sacrifices.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
  1 and 2 only | 2 and 3 only | *1 and 3 only | 1, 2 and 3
  x: In later Vedic times Prajapati became supreme and costly sacrifices dominated; Indra and Agni lost importance.

  x: Agnistoma was the regular Soma yajna. Rajasuya, Vajapeya and Ashvamedha were royal sacrifices.

  x: The Adhvaryu was associated with the Yajurveda, whereas the Udgatri was associated with the Samaveda.
[f5c390d3b8e63] sub6

  *1 | 2 | 3 | 4
  x: The 16 sanskaras are 3 before birth, 12 during life and 1 after death.

  x: Chanting belongs to the Samaveda; yajna procedures belong to the Yajurveda.
[nc5c41507bb0] sub6

  x: The Grihya Sutras governed domestic rites. The other texts belong to philosophical or theological traditions rather than domestic ritual regulations.
[nindian-history-53-5] sub7
  According to Upanishadic thought, what is the real aim of the soul?
  *Moksha (liberation) | Continuous rebirth | Svarga through sacrifice | Accumulation of karma
  x: The soul passes through a cycle of birth and death, but its real aim is liberation (Moksha) from that cycle.

  Rita is Satya | *Atman is Brahman | Prakriti is Purusha | Karma is Dharma
  x: The Upanishads identify Atman, the self, with Brahman, the cosmic principle.
[ndd32c1876c3] sub7

  x: The Brihadaranyaka Upanishad introduced the doctrine of transmigration, connected with the cycle of rebirth.

  *Meditation and the guru-disciple relationship | Temple worship | Elaborate sacrifices | Royal rituals
  x: The Upanishads criticised mechanical sacrifice and turned to meditation and instruction through a guru.

  x: Atman is the self, not body or mind, held to be one with Brahman.

  x: Samadhi is antaranga; yama, niyama, asana, pranayama and pratyahara are the five bahiranga (outer) limbs.

  3 | *5 | 6 | 8
  x: Five limbs (Yama to Pratyahara) are bahiranga; three (Dharana, Dhyana, Samadhi) are antaranga.
